Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for fitting me in. Am I good to sit right here? ... Okay, great. Thanks. 

Um, well, I guess what finally got me here is... I've just been having a little bit of a hard time at work lately. It's nothing terrible, I promise, but I'm an electrician and usually I'm really on top of my game. Just, recently, I can't seem to keep my focus on one estimate or, like, a code form anymore. It's a little embarrassing to admit, honestly. 

It's just that... a tool makes a noise on site, or I get a text, or I'll get some really great new idea about the workshop, and it just pulls me completely off track. Well, um, I wouldn't really call it being extremely restless. It's more like... I just get really driven, I guess?

Well, I mean... I wouldn't say "significant difficulties." That sounds a little harsh. I just think things have been a bit tense lately, you know?

description: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).\n  manifestation: For months I’ve been sleeping maybe three or four hours, then popping up before dawn feeling Well, "significantly impacted" is a pretty strong way to put it, I think. I mean, I'm still getting out there and doing the work, you know?

Oh, no, I wouldn't say I'm feeling a lot of stress or anxiety. I mean, sure, everyone gets a little keyed up when they've got a lot on their plate, right? But it's good energy! It's that drive you need to get things done. \n\nI guess sometimes my mind is moving so fast that I might feel a tiny bit... I don't know, restless? But it's not like a bad, anxious feeling. It's more like I'm just eager to tackle the next thing. I really don't feel like I'm struggling with it, honestly. I'm just trying to keep a positive attitude and push through. That's the best way to handle a busy schedule, don't you agree?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with focus and staying on track are affecting your overall well-being or daily life in a major way?\n\nPatient: Well, um, \"major way\" sounds a bit... I don't know, a bit heavy, right? I mean, I'm still functioning, still getting out of bed and going to work. \n\nI guess... well, I did mention the thing with the clients, right? The missed details and the scheduling getting a little tangled up. And, yeah, I suppose the guys working under me—my apprentices—they've been looking at me a little funny lately, maybe double-checking my directions more than they used to. But that's just because I'm throwing a lot of new ideas at them, you know? It's good to keep them on their toes. \n\nAnd at home... well, I know I brought up my wife being stressed. We have been arguing a bit more than usual about the mortgage and the business. She, um... she says she feels like I'm putting everything we've built at risk. Which, you know, is hard to hear, because I'm really just trying to secure our future. I'm just trying to be a good provider. That's what a husband is supposed to do, right? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing for my family.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you feel like your difficulties are affecting your relationships with your family or friends in a way that's causing you distress?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 30 / electrician / some_college\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Functional Impairment\nmanifestation: \"Clients have started complaining about missed details and mixed-up scheduling, and my apprentices don’t trust my direction the way they used to. At home, the mortgage and the business are turning into constant arguments because my wife feels like I’m putting everything we built at risk.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Distress... um. Well, I guess I just really hate feeling like I'm letting people down, you know? 

I know I keep bringing some of this stuff up, but... like with the clients starting to actually complain about the missed details and the mixed-up scheduling. I pride myself on being a good electrician, so I always want my customers to be completely happy with my work. And it does sting a little that my apprentices don't really trust my direction the way they used to. I just want us to be a solid team, and I want them to look up to me. That makes sense, right? 

And with my wife... yeah, I suppose that's the part that really gets to me. I hate fighting with her. I just want her to be happy. But it’s just turning into constant arguments at home about the mortgage and the business. She keeps saying she feels like I’m putting everything we built at risk, and... I don't know, it's just hard to hear. I just want her to be proud of what I'm doing. I'm just trying to provide a better life for us. I'm not doing the wrong thing by trying to grow the business, am I?

At home, the mortgage and the business are turning into constant arguments because my wife feels like I’m putting everything we built at risk.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I mean, my routine is still there. I still get up, I still go to the job sites. I'm not, like, falling apart or anything. I just... I guess things are a little bumpier than usual, maybe? 

I know it probably sounds like a broken record, but, um... the clients complaining about the schedule mix-ups and the missed details, that definitely throws a wrench in the day-to-day. You have to stop what you're doing to smooth things over, right? And the apprentices second-guessing me... it just slows things down when I have to explain myself twice. But I'm still managing it. I'm still getting the work done. 

And at home... well, I guess the routine there is a bit off because of the fighting. The constant arguments about the business and the mortgage... my wife saying I'm risking everything we've built... it just takes up a lot of energy. It's hard to just have a normal evening, you know? But I'm really trying to keep it all together. I'm still doing a decent job managing things, don't you think?

That's just having a solid work ethic, isn't it? I mean, people like it when you're working hard and staying ahead of the game, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant difficulties in your personal relationships due to your distractibility or impulsive behaviors?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... I wouldn't say \"significant difficulties.\" That sounds a little harsh. And maybe my apprentices have been a little... hesitant with some of my directions recently, but I just think they're having a hard time keeping up with my pace. I'm really just trying to grow the business for us. It's normal for couples to stress about money sometimes, right? I'm sure it'll all smooth over once these new projects pay off. I'm doing the right thing by trying to expand, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Have you had to turn down jobs or opportunities because you couldn't keep up with the demands or details of the project?\n\nPatient: No, no, I haven't turned down any jobs. Actually, I've been taking on more than ever! It's not about not being able to keep up; it's about prioritizing the right kind of work. That's just smart business, right? I mean, I guess things with my friends have been a little quiet lately, but that's just because I've been working so much, you know? \n\nI haven't really had the time to go grab a beer or catch a game because I'm just so focused on getting these bids out and keeping the momentum going. I think they understand, though. I mean, they know I'm trying to build something here. \n\nAnd as far as family goes, like I said, my wife gets a little stressed about the money side of things, but I wouldn't say it's because I'm impulsive or can't focus. I'm actually super focused right now! I'm just focused on a lot of different things at once. It's totally normal for guys my age to put their head down and grind for a bit, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm providing for us the best way I can, and I'm sure they all see that, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like your difficulties with focus and staying on track have significantly impacted your ability to perform your job as an electrician?\n\nPatient: Well, \"significantly impacted\" is a pretty strong way to put it, I think. I mean, I'm still getting out there and doing the work, you know? \n\nI guess... well, like I mentioned before, there have been a few little hiccups. And, yeah, maybe my apprentices have been a little... I don't know, questioning my directions lately. But that's just because I'm moving fast and thinking big, right? She just worries that I'm putting what we've built at risk, but I'm really just trying to make it better. It doesn't mean I can't perform my job, right? I'm still a good electrician, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with focus and staying on track are causing you a lot of stress or anxiety?\n\nPatient: Stress? Oh, no, I wouldn't say I'm feeling a lot of stress or anxiety. I mean, sure, everyone gets a little keyed up when they've got a lot on their plate, right? But it's good energy! It's that drive you need to get things done. \n\nI guess sometimes my mind is moving so fast that I might feel a tiny bit... I don't know, restless? But it's not like a bad, anxious feeling. It's more like I'm just eager to tackle the next thing. I really don't feel like I'm struggling with it, honestly. I'm just trying to keep a positive attitude and push through. That's the best way to handle a busy schedule, don't you agree?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with focus and staying on track are affecting your overall well-being or daily life in a major way?\n\nPatient: Well, um, \"major way\" sounds a bit... I don't know, a bit heavy, right? I mean, I'm still functioning, still getting out of bed and going to work. \n\nI guess... well, I did mention the thing with the clients, right? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing for my family.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties are affecting your relationships with your family or friends in a way that's causing you distress?\n\nPatient: Distress... um. Well, I guess I just really hate feeling like I'm letting people down, you know? \n\nI know I keep bringing some of this stuff up, but... like with the clients starting to actually complain about the missed details and the mixed-up scheduling. I pride myself on being a good electrician, so I always want my customers to be completely happy with my work. And it does sting a little that my apprentices don't really trust my direction the way they used to. I just want us to be a solid team, and I want them to look up to me. That makes sense, right? \n\nAnd with my wife... yeah, I suppose that's the part that really gets to me. I hate fighting with her. I just want her to be happy. But it’s just turning into constant arguments at home about the mortgage and the business. She keeps saying she feels like I’m putting everything we built at risk, and... I don't know, it's just hard to hear. I just want her to be proud of what I'm doing. I'm just trying to provide a better life for us. I'm not doing the wrong thing by trying to grow the business, am I?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with focus and staying on track are making it hard for you to complete daily tasks?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... I wouldn't say it's making it *hard* to complete things. I'm still getting the jobs done, you know? It's just... maybe I'm juggling a little too much at once. \n\nI know I've mentioned it already, but, um... the clients, they have brought up a few things. Just minor details I overlooked, or a schedule mix-up here and there. Nothing I can't fix! But they have complained a little. And then, well, some of my jobs started getting a little mixed up. Which really isn't like me at all, I swear. I just want to get back to doing a good job and being reliable, you know? I hope that makes sense, and that it's the kind of thing you can help with?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having trouble focusing at work?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I guess it's been... maybe about seven months? Give or take? It really hasn't been a huge problem the whole time, I promise, it's just sort of been slowly building up. \n\nThat was right around when we took on the mortgage for the new workshop and all the upgraded equipment. So, you know, it makes sense, right? Taking on a big financial commitment like that would make anyone a little scattered, don't you think? I'm sure it's just normal stress from the loan, I just... I want to make sure I'm handling it the right way so I don't let anyone down.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing or getting distracted when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, at home I'm usually just trying to relax, so it's not like I have to be super focused anyway, you know? \n\nI guess sometimes I might bounce around a little bit—like I'll start cleaning the garage and then remember I need to fix the sink, and then I'll go water the lawn. But that's just normal weekend stuff, right? Everybody does that kind of thing. It's really just at work where it's been a little bit of an issue, with the paperwork and all that. I'm sure it's nothing to worry about outside of the shop. Does that sound pretty normal to you?\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing or paying attention when you're doing hobbies or activities that you enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, when I'm doing something I like, it's pretty easy to just get into it, you know? \n\nLike, I like working on old radios, and I can sit there with a soldering iron for a while and just tinker. I guess sometimes I might get a great idea for a different project and switch gears for a bit, but that's just because I get excited about it. It's not like I'm losing focus, it's more like... I'm just enthusiastic, I guess? \n\nI'm pretty sure that's a good thing, right? To have a lot of energy for the things you enjoy? I really don't think it's a problem when it comes to my hobbies. It's just the boring work stuff that trips me up sometimes. That makes sense, doesn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse or do things without thinking them through?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I wouldn't say I do that. I mean, I try to be pretty responsible, especially with the business and everything. \n\nI guess sometimes I might make a quick decision, like... maybe buying a new tool for the shop that I didn't strictly plan for, but it's always something we'll end up needing eventually anyway, you know? It's not like I'm out there doing anything crazy or reckless. I just like to keep things moving forward. \n\nI think everyone makes snap decisions sometimes when they're busy, right? I'm pretty sure I'm just being efficient. That's a good quality for a business owner, wouldn't you say?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I can sit still just fine if I need to. \n\nI guess sometimes if I've been sitting for a really long time, like watching a movie or something, I might tap my foot or, you know, shift around in my chair a bit. But that's just because I've got a lot of energy, right? Being an electrician, I'm used to being on my feet and moving around all day, so sitting for too long just feels a little unnatural. \n\nIt's not like I'm jumping out of my skin or anything. I just prefer to be active. That's pretty normal for a guy my age who does manual labor, don't you think? It's better to be energetic than lazy, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had periods where you felt extremely restless or had trouble staying seated, even when you were doing something you enjoyed?\n\nPatient: Well, um, I wouldn't really call it being extremely restless. It's more like... I just get really driven, I guess? \n\nLike, lately, I’ve just been going nonstop. I'll bounce from job sites to writing up bids, and then I'll be right back in the workshop rewiring test panels. I mean, yeah, I guess sometimes I'll start working on a new home-automation idea before the last one is totally finished, but that's just because the ideas are flowing and sitting still just feels like... well, like I'm wasting time. \n\nWhen you have that much inspiration, you want to use it, you know? That's just having a solid work ethic, isn't it? I mean, people like it when you're working hard and staying ahead of the game, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant difficulties in your personal relationships due to your distractibility or impulsive behaviors?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... I wouldn't say \"significant difficulties.\" That sounds a little harsh. I just think things have been a bit tense lately, you know? \n\nI guess... well, okay, some of the clients have mentioned a couple of missed details here and there, or, like, a scheduling mix-up. But that happens to every business owner when they're swamped, right? And maybe my apprentices have been a little... hesitant with some of my directions recently, but I just think they're having a hard time keeping up with my pace. It's tough to find good help these days, don't you think?\n\nAnd, um, at home... well, my wife and I have had a few disagreements. She gets a little worried about the business finances and the mortgage. She says she feels like I'm putting what we've built at risk, which... I mean, I hate that she feels that way. I'm really just trying to grow the business for us. It's normal for couples to stress about money sometimes, right? I'm sure it'll all smooth over once these new projects pay off. I'm doing the right thing by trying to expand, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Have you had to turn down jobs or opportunities because you couldn't keep up with the demands or details of the project?\n\nPatient: No, no, I haven't turned down any jobs. Actually, I've been taking on more than ever! I mean, I guess there was one small residential job last week that I passed on, but that was just because it was a tiny fix-it thing, and I'm really focusing on the bigger, more innovative projects right now. \n\nI want to make sure I'm putting my energy where it counts the most, you know? It's not about not being able to keep up; it's about prioritizing the right kind of work. That's just smart business, right? You have to focus on the projects that are going to move you forward, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any negative impact on your relationships with friends or family due to your difficulties with focus or impulsivity?\n\nPatient: Well, um, not really. I mean, I guess things with my friends have been a little quiet lately, but that's just because I've been working so much, you know? \n\nI haven't really had the time to go grab a beer or catch a game because I'm just so focused on getting these bids out and keeping the momentum going. I think they understand, though. I mean, they know I'm trying to build something here. \n\nAnd as far as family goes, like I said, my wife gets a little stressed about the money side of things, but I wouldn't say it's because I'm impulsive or can't focus. I'm actually super focused right now! I'm just focused on a lot of different things at once. It's totally normal for guys my age to put their head down and grind for a bit, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm providing for us the best way I can, and I'm sure they all see that, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like your difficulties with focus and staying on track have significantly impacted your ability to perform your job as an electrician?\n\nPatient: Well, \"significantly impacted\" is a pretty strong way to put it, I think. I mean, I'm still getting out there and doing the work, you know? \n\nI guess... well, like I mentioned before, there have been a few little hiccups. You know, a couple of clients did bring up some missed details, and there was that scheduling thing. But that's just because I'm trying to juggle so much to grow the business. And, yeah, maybe my apprentices have been a little... I don't know, questioning my directions lately. But that's just because I'm moving fast and thinking big, right? It's not that I can't do the job; it's just that I'm trying to do a *lot* of the job all at once. \n\nAnd, I mean, the stuff at home with my wife... the arguments about the mortgage and the business... that's just the stress of being an entrepreneur, don't you think? She just worries that I'm putting what we've built at risk, but I'm really just trying to make it better. It doesn't mean I can't perform my job, right? I'm still a good electrician, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with focus and staying on track are causing you a lot of stress or anxiety?\n\nPatient: Stress? Oh, no, I wouldn't say I'm feeling a lot of stress or anxiety. I mean, sure, everyone gets a little keyed up when they've got a lot on their plate, right? But it's good energy! It's that drive you need to get things done. \n\nI guess sometimes my mind is moving so fast that I might feel a tiny bit... I don't know, restless? But it's not like a bad, anxious feeling. It's more like I'm just eager to tackle the next thing. I really don't feel like I'm struggling with it, honestly. I'm just trying to keep a positive attitude and push through. That's the best way to handle a busy schedule, don't you agree?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with focus and staying on track are affecting your overall well-being or daily life in a major way?\n\nPatient: Well, um, \"major way\" sounds a bit... I don't know, a bit heavy, right? I mean, I'm still functioning, still getting out of bed and going to work. \n\nI guess... well, I did mention the thing with the clients, right? The missed details and the scheduling getting a little tangled up. And, yeah, I suppose the guys working under me—my apprentices—they've been looking at me a little funny lately, maybe double-checking my directions more than they used to. But that's just because I'm throwing a lot of new ideas at them, you know? It's good to keep them on their toes. \n\nAnd at home... well, I know I brought up my wife being stressed. We have been arguing a bit more than usual about the mortgage and the business. She, um... she says she feels like I'm putting everything we've built at risk. Which, you know, is hard to hear, because I'm really just trying to secure our future. I'm just trying to be a good provider. That's what a husband is supposed to do, right? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing for my family.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties are affecting your relationships with your family or friends in a way that's causing you distress?\n\nPatient: Distress... um. Well, I guess I just really hate feeling like I'm letting people down, you know? \n\nI know I keep bringing some of this stuff up, but... like with the clients starting to actually complain about the missed details and the mixed-up scheduling. I pride myself on being a good electrician, so I always want my customers to be completely happy with my work. And it does sting a little that my apprentices don't really trust my direction the way they used to. I just want us to be a solid team, and I want them to look up to me. That makes sense, right? \n\nAnd with my wife... yeah, I suppose that's the part that really gets to me. I hate fighting with her. I just want her to be happy. But it’s just turning into constant arguments at home about the mortgage and the business. She keeps saying she feels like I’m putting everything we built at risk, and... I don't know, it's just hard to hear. I just want her to be proud of what I'm doing. I'm just trying to provide a better life for us. I'm not doing the wrong thing by trying to grow the business, am I?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with focus and staying on track are making it hard for you to complete daily tasks?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... I wouldn't say it's making it *hard* to complete things. I'm still getting the jobs done, you know? It's just... maybe I'm juggling a little too much at once. \n\nI know I've mentioned it already, but, um... the clients, they have brought up a few things. Just minor details I overlooked, or a schedule mix-up here and there. Nothing I can't fix! But they have complained a little. And my apprentices, they're just... they're second-guessing my directions lately. Which I guess means I might not be communicating as clearly as I think I am? I just want to be a good boss to them. \n\nAnd, well, the daily stuff at home... I guess the arguments with my wife are taking up a lot of time. It's just constant back-and-forth about the mortgage and how I'm running the business. She really feels like I'm risking everything we've built, and that's... that's definitely making it harder to just relax and get through the day normally. But I'm still keeping things running, for the most part. I'm doing okay, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with focus and staying on track are interfering with your ability to manage your daily routine?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, my routine is still there. I still get up, I still go to the job sites. I'm not, like, falling apart or anything. I just... I guess things are a little bumpier than usual, maybe? \n\nI know it probably sounds like a broken record, but, um... the clients complaining about the schedule mix-ups and the missed details, that definitely throws a wrench in the day-to-day. I'm still getting the work done. \n\nAnd at home... well, I guess the routine there is a bit off because of the fighting. But I'm really trying to keep it all together. I'm still doing a decent job managing things, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with focus and staying on track are significantly affecting your daily life?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say \"significantly.\" I mean, I'm still functioning, you know? I'm still showing up and trying my best. It's not like my whole life is falling apart. \n\nI know I've brought this up a few times, so I'm sorry if I'm repeating myself, but... yeah, I guess there are some hiccups. I'm working on it.\n\nAnd then, you know, the stuff with my wife. But I'm really trying to balance it all and make everyone happy. I'm really not letting it get me down. I'm just trying to take it all in stride. That's the right way to handle it, isn't it? Just keeping a good attitude?\n\nDoctor: Are your difficulties with focus and staying on track making it hard for you to enjoy your daily activities?\n\nPatient: Enjoy my activities? Um, no, I think I'm still enjoying things. I mean, I love being an electrician. It's a great trade. Even when things get a little hectic, I still like the work itself. \n\nI guess maybe I'm a little more tired at the end of the day, so I might not do as much when I get home, but... I still like watching the game or, you know, just relaxing. The focus stuff hasn't ruined that for me. I'm still finding the good in the day-to-day. That's important, right?     "psychosocial_stressor": true,
```json
    "additional_requirements": [
      "symptoms have been present in two or more settings (work and, to some extent, home)",
      "there is clear evidence of clinically significant distress or impairment in social, occupational, or other important areas of functioning, as indicated by problems with clients, apprentices, and marital stress"
    ]
  }
}
```
=====================================
